# What I Want to Call Love-Driven Development β

<!--PART:Part 1|The question, and the views so far-->

## What drives a project?

<!--OPEN-->

After a year of exploring the shapes things take as they come into being, and human consciousness, one question remained.

<a id="ldd-question"></a><strong>pjdhiro's question (translated by Claude):</strong>What drives us to do projects? What drives us to live?

Businesses and projects are usually told starting from a vision. The picture is that there are first a purpose and a plan, and people move along them.

About the results of design and vision, pjdhiro feels the following.

<strong>pjdhiro's experience (translated by Claude):</strong>…It seems as if I am trying to make it, but it is born as if the field were swirling into a vortex. I feel so many driving sources that I cannot tell whether it is me or the environment. Is it causation? Is it correlation?

Looking back, it seems as though something had begun to move before the vision was put into words. pjdhiro sees this feeling of dots connecting within one person as close to what Steve Jobs talked about in a [Stanford commencement speech](https://news.stanford.edu/stories/2005/06/youve-got-find-love-jobs-says) (2005). After dropping out of college, he sat in on a calligraphy class because he found it interesting, with no prospect of it ever being useful, and ten years later it became the beautiful typography of the first Macintosh. Jobs said that you cannot connect the dots looking forward; you can only connect them looking backward.

<a id="ldd-drive"></a>On this page, whatever moves people and projects is called the **driving source**. The first question asks the "what" of the driving source, and the other question asks the "why".

<a id="ldd-question-why"></a><strong>pjdhiro's question (translated by Claude):</strong>Why do we try to become something different from what we were?

With this "try to become", pjdhiro wants to say, without separating them, both the passive sense of becoming without meaning to and the active sense of seeming to try to become. The first half of this reader (Parts 1 to 5) looks at the first question, and the second half (Part 6) at the second. Part 7 bundles the two together and looks at what what I want to call Love-Driven Development is.

<details><summary>More — what starts moving before a vision is put into words</summary>

The movement before a vision is put into words takes several forms. Within one person, dots may connect afterward, as if preparation had already begun. Often the field itself is fluctuating, as with social movements and trends, and people start to move riding that fluctuation, or sometimes betting against it. People being drawn together and something beginning is one case among these.

</details>

<details><summary>More — the path management studies have written, and what is not yet known</summary>

Management studies and project management have described in detail the path that follows once a vision has been put into words: set the purpose, make a plan, gather people and resources, check progress and correct course. Such descriptions work as they are in many situations. Which way of thinking has treated what as the driving source is listed in the next chapter, "[What has management studies seen as the driving source?](#ldd-management)".

About the vision itself, the 5W1H are still not well understood: why it arises, and where, in whom, when, from what and how. Whether business opportunities are discovered or created is still debated in entrepreneurship research (in 2000 Shane and Venkataraman framed the field around the discovery, evaluation and exploitation of opportunities, and in 2007 Alvarez and Barney set side by side two views, discovery and creation).

Looking back, many people have experienced dots connecting before a vision, as if preparation had already begun, or being moved by fluctuations in the field. What Love-Driven Development tries to see is something like a law that works on people, beyond their control, behind such things happening. For now it approaches the "why" of this from the angle of the driving source.

<!--REF:Management studies and entrepreneurship research-->

Sources: [Shane & Venkataraman (2000)](https://doi.org/10.5465/amr.2000.2791611) "The promise of entrepreneurship as a field of research", *Academy of Management Review* 25(1):217 (full text checked against [a copy of the journal pages on a University of Liverpool server](https://indico.ph.liv.ac.uk/event/1424/sessions/1150/attachments/3345/4673/Shane%20and%20Venkataraman-PromiseEntrepreneurshipField-2000.pdf); definition of the field p.218) / [Alvarez & Barney (2007)](https://doi.org/10.1002/sej.4) "Discovery and creation: alternative theories of entrepreneurial action", *Strategic Entrepreneurship Journal* 1(1–2):11 (full text checked against [a copy of the journal pages on the site of the research group effectuation.org](https://effectuation.org/hubfs/Journal%20Articles/2017/05/Alvarez_et_al-2007-Strategic_Entrepreneurship_Journal.pdf); discovery theory and creation theory p.11).

</details>

<details class="pd-column" id="ldd-dots"><summary><span>Dots connect looking backward<span class="pd-column__sub">Steve Jobs's speech</span></span></summary>

To the dots that connect looking backward, pjdhiro adds the following image.

<blockquote class="reader-callout reader-request">
<p><strong>pjdhiro's image (translated by Claude):</strong>Heading toward the North Star, circling around it — that is one image of what I want to call Love.</p>
</blockquote>

</details>

## What has management studies seen as the driving source?

<!--OPEN-->

When explaining Love-Driven Development, pjdhiro has put answering the following question at the core of the explanation.

<a id="ldd-management"></a><strong>pjdhiro's question (translated by Claude):</strong>What is the driving source of a project?

In England's Premier League, Leicester City won the league in 2016 under manager Claudio Ranieri. Nine months later, with 13 games left, the team was one point above the relegation zone, and the club dismissed Ranieri. In its statement, the club wrote that there was no doubt Ranieri was the most successful manager in the club's history.

What was different between the year of the title and the next year? Did key players leave, did opponents study the team, did the goals or the training plans change, did the manager's enthusiasm spin its wheels, or was the title year just lucky? Many explanations come up, and each seems a little right. Still, no single one is enough, and even adding them together, something remains that cannot be fully explained about how much things changed under the same manager and the same club tradition.

Management studies, too, has seen the driving source in many places: markets and competition, a company's resources, goals and plans, learning from action, organizational culture, people's motivation. The explanations listed for Leicester each correspond to one of these driving sources.

<div class="tablefit cols-2">

| Explanation listed | Driving source in management studies |
|---|---|
| Key players left | A company's resources |
| Opponents studied the team | Markets and competition |
| The goals or the training plans changed | Goals and plans |
| The manager's enthusiasm spun its wheels | People's motivation |

</div>

Each way of thinking works well in situations that suit it. Yet laid on top of one another, they feel like the parable of the blind men and the elephant: each surely touches part of the elephant, and still something seems missing. Going down from the outside inward, one finally arrives at the entrepreneur's mind. What Love-Driven Development follows is what lies further behind that mind and moves it.

The table below sorts representative ways of thinking into seven groups by what they see as the driving source. They are ordered from the external environment toward the inside of the person, and the right-hand column gives the situations where each works well.

<div class="tablefit cols-3">

| Driving source | Representative ways of thinking | Situations where it works |
|---|---|---|
| External environment (markets, competition, customers, stakeholders, the wider environment) | Shareholder value maximization, competitive strategy, disruptive innovation, marketing (Kotler), stakeholder theory, the environmental school | Right as long as the forces coming from outside line up with the direction people are drawn in |
| Internal resources | Resource-based view (VRIO) | Right as long as the people who make use of the resources are drawn to them |
| An endpoint decided in advance (goals, plans, efficiency) | Management by objectives (MBO), OKR, business planning, scientific management, lean production | Right as long as one does not ask where the endpoint came from |
| Action and learning | Lean startup, real options, emergent strategy | Right as long as what to test and where to move keep arising |
| What a group shares (norms, culture, purpose) | Business ethics, compliance, CSR, organizational culture, knowledge creation theory (Nonaka), purpose-driven management | Right as long as the group is open and what is shared keeps arising |
| The inside of the person (empathy, motivation, judgment) | Design thinking, motivation theories, rational choice | Right as long as one does not ask where empathy and motivation come from |
| The entrepreneur's mind (vision, desire, instinct) | The entrepreneurial school, effectuation | Right as long as one does not ask what moves that mind |

</div>

<details><summary>More — how to read the table of seven, and sources</summary>

When people look only at the part they touched and think the whole elephant is there, words turn into hollow buzzwords. What Love-Driven Development tries to do is touch the parts not yet touched.

In the entrepreneur's mind, vision, desire and instinct all surely work as driving sources. The careers of Kotler and Ikujiro Nonaka, who kept updating their theories for decades, also seem to show their center of gravity shifting toward people's feelings and values.

A table that looks at each way of thinking in the table, by the situations where it works well and the misreadings likely when it is spread as the right answer, is in [How far do the ideas of management studies work?](management-drivers.html) (in Japanese).

<!--REF:Management studies and entrepreneurship research-->

Sources: Mintzberg, Ahlstrand & Lampel (1998) *Strategy Safari* (full text checked, in translation, against the [Wayback Machine record of the publisher's reading sample](https://web.archive.org/web/20191020102857/https://www.m-vg.de/mediafiles/Leseprobe/9783898796750.pdf) (20 October 2019) of the German translation *Strategy Safari. Der Wegweiser durch den Dschungel des strategischen Managements*, tr. J. T. A. Wegberg, FinanzBuch Verlag, 2012; Saxe's poem at the opening, pp.15–17) / Saxe "The Blind Men and the Elephant" (full text checked against the public-domain edition of *The Poems of John Godfrey Saxe* (1872) [on Wikisource](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/The_Poems_of_John_Godfrey_Saxe/The_Blind_Men_and_the_Elephant); scans 279–281) / Kotler, Kartajaya & Setiawan (2010) *Marketing 3.0* ([full text checked against the publisher Wiley's excerpt; chapter 1 p.4](https://media.wiley.com/product_data/excerpt/24/04705988/0470598824.pdf)) and (2021) *Marketing 5.0* ([full text checked against the publisher Wiley's excerpt; chapter 1 pp.3, 5](https://catalogimages.wiley.com/images/db/pdf/9781119668510.excerpt.pdf)) / Nonaka & Katsumi (2020) *Kyōkan keiei* [Empathy management] (in Japanese; full text checked against the [introduction published on Nikkei BOOK Plus on 20 January 2023](https://bookplus.nikkei.com/atcl/column/032900009/011300261/) by Nikkei BP, the publisher's parent company). The grouping of the table and the "Situations where it works" column are Claude's arrangement. / The Leicester City example: UEFA, "[Premier League champions Leicester sack Ranieri](https://www.uefa.com/uefachampionsleague/news/0236-0e96579ac381-9a18124c9a02-1000--premier-league-champions-leicester-sack-ranieri)" (23 February 2017; full text checked: the dismissal nine months after the title, one point above the relegation zone with 13 games left, the club's statement).

</details>

<details class="pd-column" id="ldd-blind-elephant"><summary>The blind men and the elephant</summary>

There is more than one way to see strategy. Mintzberg, Ahlstrand and Lampel put Saxe's poem "The Blind Men and the Elephant" at the opening of *Strategy Safari* (1998). In the poem, blind men touch an elephant; the one who touches the trunk says it is like a snake, the one who touches the knee says it is like a tree. It ends by saying that each was partly in the right, and all were in the wrong. (See also: [Mintzberg's schools, seen through driving sources and the flow of creation](mintzberg-schools.html) (in Japanese))

The origin and spread of the parable itself are explained in the Japanese Wikipedia article "[Gunmō zō o hyōsu](https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E7%BE%A4%E7%9B%B2%E8%B1%A1%E3%82%92%E8%A9%95%E3%81%99)" [The blind men and the elephant] (in Japanese). The story has been cited in religion and in science as well, to show how people declare the whole from one aspect alone.

The Indian religious teacher Ramakrishna told this story at the gathering for his birthday on 11 March 1883, when admonishing people who were arguing that only their own sect was right. He says that someone who has seen only one aspect of God limits God to that aspect.

The physicist Bohm cited the story in his textbook *Quantum Theory* (1951), where he explains the particle and wave behavior of light ([How far does it resemble physics in structure?](#ldd-physics)).

Idries Shah rewrote the story as one in which scientists keep debating in front of a magnified image of a surface like skin, and as the camera pulls back, it turns out to be an elephant. From Shah's script, Richard Williams made it into a short animated film in 1965. The story is included as the title story of the 1970 book *The Dermis Probe*.

<!--REF:Management studies and entrepreneurship research-->

Sources: The Ramakrishna story: *The Gospel of Sri Ramakrishna*, M's (Mahendranath Gupta's) record translated into English by Swami Nikhilananda (English translation 1942; the edition of Sri Ramakrishna Math, Madras, full text checked against [the copy in the Digital Library of India](https://archive.org/details/in.ernet.dli.2015.462256); the entry for 11 March 1883, p.191) / Bohm (1951) *Quantum Theory*, Prentice-Hall (the Dover reprint [an unabridged republication of the 1951 edition], full text checked against [the publisher's preview on Google Books](https://books.google.co.jp/books?id=GKVGDwAAQBAJ&pg=PA26); p.26) / Shah (1970) *The Dermis Probe* (full text checked against [the 2016 edition that The Idries Shah Foundation publishes free of charge](https://idriesshahfoundation.org/pdfviewer/the-dermis-probe/); note on the film p.xi, the title story pp.1–3).

</details>

## What does Love-Driven Development see as the driving source?

<!--OPEN-->

So far, the ways of thinking in management studies have been lined up from this reader's own viewpoint: what each treats as the driving source. Then how does Love-Driven Development differ from them?

<a id="ldd-what"></a><strong>pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):</strong>Until now, the concepts of a project have been grouped in ways like moving people by goals (management by objectives), by their motivation (motivation theory), or by a vision (vision-led management). I think I went one step further and looked for why those arise.

Among these groupings so far, the view of moving people by goals holds that people move if the purpose is clear. The view of moving people by motivation holds that motivated people drive a project. The two are different views, but both assume that people who have a purpose or motivation go in that direction. Yet it happens that a person decides their own purpose, is full of motivation, and still ends up somewhere entirely different.

When he was young, Vincent van Gogh believed he was called to preach, and became an evangelist in the Borinage, a coal-mining region of Belgium. He was so devoted that he stayed up through the night beside the sick and injured, and gave away most of his clothes to others. Even so, after a trial period, the church did not keep him on as an evangelist. The reason was that he lacked the ability to speak in front of people. In a letter to his brother after losing the post, Van Gogh wrote, "there is something inside me, what can it be!" (translated by Claude). In a letter three months later, he wrote that since he had picked up the pencil he had put aside and decided to return to drawing, everything seemed to have changed. From here, Van Gogh went on drawing and painting.

Both the purpose and the motivation were real. Even so, what moved Van Gogh seems to lie further behind purpose and motivation, something that even he could only call "something".

<a id="ldd-answer"></a>The driving source of Love-Driven Development is what I want to call Love.

On this page, Love is not limited to romantic love, and for now it is placed in two aspects: the aspect of a force by which people and things feel drawn, and the aspect of a property of the field that people are in. pjdhiro sees both as right, and each as one aspect, like what each of the men who touched the elephant said in the parable of the blind men and the elephant in the previous chapter. The driving sources in [the management studies table](#ldd-management) were things of a size people can see with their eyes and talk about in words, like markets, goals, motivation and the entrepreneur's mind. What I want to call Love is what moves those from behind them. Love-Driven Development is the name pjdhiro wants to give, all together, to what is overlooked on the back side of the phenomenon called a project: this driving source, and the structures and processes it gives rise to.

<details><summary>More — what moves behind, before a vision is put into words</summary>

Something begins to be born while there is not yet a vision put into words or a shared intention. Within one person, in a group of people, and in the flow of society, such movements happen before a vision is put into words.

In Part 5, the chapter "[What aspects does what I want to call Love have?](#ldd-love-faces)" looks at these two aspects alongside other aspects.

</details>

<details><summary>More — how to point to something that cannot be seen</summary>

Answering "It is Love" conveys almost nothing. What lies before a vision cannot be observed directly. The word love, too, is easily read through its easily recognized aspects, such as romance and liking.

<a id="ldd-overlap"></a>So this page writes the way physics has written about gravity. It piles up phenomena that are moved by what I want to call Love, and the words that have described them. Each one, like a circle, encloses a certain range. When many circles are laid over one another, the overlap common to all of them remains: in a Venn diagram, the AND of all the circles. That overlap shows where what I want to call Love is.

<a id="ldd-four"></a>The circles this page lays over one another are mainly four explorations. Over this year, pjdhiro has continued "[What Is Creation](https://uminomae.github.io/project-design-theory/cs/)", "[What Is Consciousness](https://uminomae.github.io/project-design-theory/as/)" and "[Kesson-Driven Thinking](https://uminomae.github.io/project-design-theory/ks/)", and the reader "[Isn't Love like gravity?](gravity-love.html)" (in Japanese). Like gravity, each is an exploration that has described, from a different side, how the things being moved move. This page calls them the **four explorations**, and calls on them one at a time where needed in the flow of the story.

</details>

<details class="pd-column"><summary>The finger pointing at the moon</summary>

<a id="ldd-finger"></a>pjdhiro thinks that the more clearly something is expressed, the more it is misunderstood one-sidedly. As in the Buddhist parable of the finger pointing at the moon (the finger pointing at the moon is not the moon itself; *Śūraṅgama Sūtra* fascicle 2, *Dà zhìdù lùn* fascicle 9), the words and hypotheses on this page are not what I want to call Love itself, but fingers pointing at it.

</details>

<details><summary>More — gravity has been described by the things it moves</summary>

There is a precedent for describing what cannot be seen: physics. No one has seen gravity itself. Even so, it has been inferred that a falling apple and the orbiting moon follow the same law, and, from how gas and stars on the outskirts of galaxies rotate, that there is mass not visible in light. Gravity has been described by how the things it moves move. In the image of a black hole (published in 2019), the center itself does not appear either. What appears is a dark shadow and the ring of light that rims it. Where the center is can be told from the surrounding light.

<!--REF:Physics and the history of science-->

Sources: [Rubin & Ford (1970)](https://doi.org/10.1086/150317) *ApJ* 159:379 (full text checked against the scan of the journal pages made public by NASA ADS; abstract p.379, rotation curve flat in the outer parts p.392) / [Rubin, Ford & Thonnard (1980)](https://doi.org/10.1086/158003) *ApJ* 238:471 (galaxy rotation curves; full text checked against the scan of the journal pages made public by NASA ADS; abstract on p.471 on mass beyond the optical image) / [Event Horizon Telescope Collaboration (2019)](https://doi.org/10.3847/2041-8213/ab0ec7) *ApJL* 875:L1 (the image of M87; full text checked against the journal version (CC BY) on arXiv 1906.11238; abstract on p.1 on the ring of light and the dark center) / [*Śūraṅgama Sūtra* fascicle 2](https://21dzk.l.u-tokyo.ac.jp/SAT2018/satdb2018pre.php?mode=detail&ob=1&mode2=2&useid=0945_,19,0111a) (Taishō T19 No.945, p.111a) and [*Dà zhìdù lùn* fascicle 9](https://21dzk.l.u-tokyo.ac.jp/SAT2018/satdb2018pre.php?mode=detail&ob=1&mode2=2&useid=1509_,25,0125b) (Taishō T25 No.1509, p.125b) (both full text checked against the SAT Daizōkyō Text Database).

</details>

<details><summary>More — a structure, a viewpoint, a principle, or a process?</summary>

While thinking about where to place Love-Driven Development within existing fields such as the study of business conception, pjdhiro asked again what this name points to in the first place.

<strong>pjdhiro's question (translated by Claude):</strong>Am I talking about the structure of a project, or a viewpoint for looking at projects, or the principle of why projects are born and move, or a design process?

Placed side by side, the four explorations do not fit into any single one of these. The driving source is close to the principle of why things are born and move. [The gravity well](#ldd-well) (a picture of a gravitational field drawn as a hollow around a mass) is close to a structure, of what is where. [Field → wave → edge → vortex → bundle](#ldd-vortex) (the five stages of creation set out in "What Is Creation") is close to a process, of how things proceed. [The work of dividing oneself](#ldd-center) (observing one's own feelings and thoughts as objects through metacognition) is close to a viewpoint, of where one looks from.

They can be read as the same single thing seen from different sides: explorations that have touched, from different sides, something invisible that moves before a vision, and that keeps moving beneath it after it is put into words.

</details>

<details class="pd-column"><summary>What this page writes, and how</summary>

<a id="ldd-method"></a>pjdhiro has experienced many times the phenomenon pjdhiro wants to call Love-Driven Development. From that experience, this page puts into words a viewpoint, a stance and a way of working toward projects. What it deals with is the driving source of projects. Projects are not limited to business; they include life and family. Such human activities are a domain sociology has broadly taken as its subject, and management studies and economics have dealt with part of it. This page reads that domain from the angle of the driving source, from the viewpoint of psychology. In doing so, it draws on structural similarities with physics (gravity and quantum theory) and on findings from neurophenomenology (a way of looking that sets descriptions of experience against findings on how nerves work).

Put another way: when a project moves, people are thinking and feeling something. This page looks at that in light of how nerves work. Nerves, too, are made of the same matter as everything else in the universe and follow the same laws of physics. So when the way thinking and feeling move is set beside the behavior of gravity and quanta, there are places where the shapes look similar. This page uses those similar shapes as clues to infer the invisible driving source.

Much of the knowledge cited here is what the four explorations have each researched. The papers and books cited are gathered in "[Sources and references](#ldd-refs)" at the end, and the pages of the four explorations in "[Links](#ldd-links)".

This page also has the aspect of confirming, from the viewpoint of Western science, what has long been taught in Eastern thought, because that is how pjdhiro has thought.

</details>

<details class="pd-column"><summary>When someone else decides the purpose</summary>

It is sometimes thought that if someone else decides the purpose, people will move toward it. Child-rearing in which parents decide the path their child will take is one example. Charles Darwin's father had his son study medicine in Edinburgh to make him a doctor, and when he learned that it did not suit him, sent him to Cambridge to make him a clergyman. In his autobiography, Darwin wrote that the medical lectures were intolerably dull, and also that his father scolded him for caring about nothing but shooting, dogs and rat-catching. What absorbed him at Cambridge was collecting beetles. He wrote that once, holding a rare beetle in each hand, he saw a third, new kind, and not wanting to lose it, put the one in his right hand into his mouth. The path to becoming a clergyman, Darwin wrote, died a natural death when he sailed on the Beagle as a naturalist after graduating.

</details>

## What has the word Love pointed to?

<!--OPEN-->

The words "ai" and "Love" are often read first as the feeling of romantic love. Even the everyday word for liking someone, *suki*, has several kinds. When you say you like your friends, like a celebrity, or like your family, the same "like" has different contents. With friends, you feel you want to be together as equals. A celebrity you feel you want to support, even if you can never meet them. Family you feel you do not want to lose, even when you fight.

Even saying "what I want to call Love", what the word Love points to differs by person and era. When looking at what Love is, pjdhiro first asked Claude this.

<a id="ldd-love-history"></a><strong>pjdhiro's request (translated by Claude):</strong>So that it's clear I'm dealing with LOVE in a broad range, first sort out the existing kinds of Love.

Just as everyday "liking" divides into kinds, the words that have been translated as Japanese *ai* or English love were different words in the sources.

Moreover, Love in the sources has not been written only as a feeling directed at people. What it is written as is not one thing: a movement of the mind, a mind or practice to be cultivated, a desire seeking what is lacking, a relationship, a principle or force that moves the world, God itself. What it is directed at also differs from source to source: from one particular person to all living beings, God, the makeup of the world. Nor has it been written only as something good; it has also been written as the root of suffering and the seed of deeds deserving punishment. Modern dictionaries line these up under one word, which shows that the single word Love has been applied to a very broad range.

Who called what Love, and when, is gathered in detail, from the full text of the sources, on the separate page "[How has Love been spoken of?](love-history.html)" (in Japanese; under construction), in the order of the Buddhist stream, the stream of Western philosophy, and modern dictionaries, psychology and neuroscience. pjdhiro thinks that what is closest, for now, to what pjdhiro wants to call Love is what is in Peirce's "[evolution by creative love](peirce-love-abduction.html)" (in Japanese).

<details><summary>More — words, ways of writing, objects, good and bad, dictionaries</summary>

What is lined up here is Love as defined by people other than pjdhiro. What pjdhiro wants to call Love is seen from the Part 5 chapter "[What I want to call Love](#ldd-love)" on.

**It was not one word.** The words that have been translated as Japanese *ai* or English love are different words in the sources. In early Buddhism, craving (taṇhā), held to be the cause of suffering, and loving-kindness (mettā), held to be a mind to be cultivated, are different words. In Greek, eros is used by Plato, friendship (philia) by Aristotle and agape by the New Testament: different sources. The *Mencius* distinguishes three words, *ai*, *ren* and *qin*, by whom they are directed at.

**What it is written as is not one thing either.** It is not limited to what is written as an emotion.

<div class="tablefit cols-2">

| Written as | Examples |
|---|---|
| A movement or state of the mind | Craving that becomes the root of suffering (early Buddhism). Joy accompanied by the idea of an external cause (Spinoza). A state of intense longing for union with another (Hatfield and Sprecher) |
| A mind or practice to be cultivated | A mind extended without limit to all living beings (the *Metta Sutta*). A mind that gives happiness and removes suffering (*Dà zhìdù lùn*). A practice that comes out as words (Dōgen's loving speech). A commandment (the New Testament) |
| A desire seeking what is lacking | Socrates' dialogue in Plato's *Symposium* |
| A relationship | Friendship wishing each other good (Aristotle). The child's tie to the mother (Bowlby) |
| A principle or force that moves the world | The working that gathers the four roots into one (Empedocles). The love that moves the sun and the other stars (Dante). A mode of evolution (Peirce; the separate page "[Peirce's creative love, and abduction](peirce-love-abduction.html)" (in Japanese)) |
| A weight | The weight that carries oneself (Augustine) |
| God itself | "God is love" (the First Epistle of John) |
| A combination of components | The three of intimacy, passion and decision/commitment (Sternberg) |

</div>

**What it is directed at also differs from source to source.** One particular person, someone with whom one wishes each other good, those extended in order from the near, all living beings without boundaries, God, things that are not human, oneself, the makeup of the world.

**Nor has it been written only as something good.** The craving of early Buddhism is the root from which suffering arises. The *Dhammapada* writes that from what is dear arise sorrow and fear. Dante writes that love is the seed both of every virtue and of every deed deserving punishment. Augustine writes that two loves made two cities.

**Modern dictionaries line these up under one word.** The *Daijisen* divides *ai* into six: the affection of parents and children or siblings, the heart that holds a particular person dear, the feeling of liking things, a heart wishing for happiness beyond personal feeling, the loving-kindness of God in Christianity, and the craving of Buddhism. *Ren'ai* (romantic love) is a word used as the translation of love from the beginning of the Meiji era. Psychology has divided love into measurable forms such as behavior, states and components. Neuroscience research measures the brain activity of people in love, but does not write that some part of the brain is the seat of love.

</details>

<!--TURN-->

Part 1 posed the question and checked the views so far on the driving source of projects and on the word Love. This reader sees that driving source as what I want to call Love.

What I want to call Love cannot be seen with the eyes. Part 2 looks at the invisible driving source through things similar in shape: the structure of how things come into being, and gravity. At the end, it looks at how far the borrowed shapes of physics resemble it.

<!--/TURN-->

<!--PART:Part 2|Seeing the invisible driving source through similar shapes-->

<!--LENS:About this part|-->

Among what was lined up in the chapter at the end of Part 1, "[What has the word Love pointed to?](#ldd-love-history)", there were writings that put Love not only as a human feeling but as a principle or force that moves the world: the working that gathers the four roots into one (Empedocles), and the love that moves the sun and the other stars (Dante). Love has also been written as if it worked even on the universe and on matter.

On the other hand, if we group under creation the coming into being of things, and their becoming different from what they were, similar structures are reported both in the domains of matter and in the domains of mind and society. The shape of creation may be similar whether or not a thing is material. If so, might the structure and process by which what I want to call Love comes into being have the same shape too? This question lies beneath Part 2.

From these two, Part 2 thinks about Love from the side of matter. Physics may feel like the thing farthest from Love. Even so, to see the invisible driving source, it first borrows things similar in shape. There are three chapters.

- "[What shape does creation take?](#what-shape-does-creation-take)": the structure of how things come into being (field → wave → edge → vortex → bundle). It is the basic shape for seeing the shape in which Love comes into being.
- "[Isn't Love like gravity?](#isnt-love-like-gravity)": that a force that draws things can also be written as a distortion of a field. It is for setting side by side Love's two faces: the force one feels drawn by, and the character of a field.
- "[How far does it resemble physics in structure?](#how-far-does-it-resemble-physics-in-structure)": it gathers the borrowed shapes of physics, and looks at how far they resemble it and where they stop resembling it.

<!--/LENS-->

## What shape does creation take?

<!--OPEN-->

Part 2 first looks at the structure of how things come into being. One of the explorations, "What Is Creation", writes about that structure. The shape of creation continues as a basic viewpoint into the second half of this reader, so an overview is placed first.

"What Is Creation" holds that creation proceeds field → wave → edge → vortex → bundle. Its top page sets out the five stages like this: "The field is a stable, sea-like state before waves rise. The wave is the situation changing, and differences splitting and facing each other. The edge is the border where heterogeneous things meet and collide. The vortex is gathering as if one, while the two can still be told apart. The bundle is a gathering that remains as a new form, and it becomes the next field again" (translated by Claude). It also writes that this definition is provisional, and will be reinforced by building in features found in the research.

pjdhiro also rephrases the five stages with an image of the sea. In the sequence sea → wave → trough between waves → vortex → ocean current, the field is the sea, the wave is the wave, the edge is the trough between waves, the vortex is the vortex, and the bundle is the ocean current (the "sea analogy" column of the [table of the five stages](https://uminomae.github.io/project-design-theory/cs/reader/five-stages.html) in "What Is Creation", in Japanese).

Shapes similar to this have been reported in [30 academic domains](https://uminomae.github.io/project-design-theory/cs/?modal=domains) (in Japanese).

<a id="ldd-field"></a>From here on, the word "field" is used for both the field of the five stages of creation and the field of physics: what spreads beneath, where a vortex swirls.

<figure class="glow-card reader-figure"><img src="../assets/img/creation-five-stages-v1-3.jpg" alt="Five Stages of Creation: structure and process (labels in Japanese). Field: a stable, sea-like state before waves rise; the starting point in theory. Wave: differences arise in the field and rise as oscillation; the situation changes, and differences split and face each other. Edge: the border where heterogeneous things meet and collide; new relations and laws are born there. Vortex: rises as one whole; the two can be told apart, yet are as if one. Bundle: vortices affect one another and remain as a gathering in a new form, which becomes the next field. The table below matches structure and process: field = nothingness (hard to perceive) and drifting; wave = oscillation, opposition and separation; edge = boundary, relation, meeting and collision; vortex = the individual, rising and inclusion; bundle = direction and collection. The five stages do not necessarily proceed in order; they run at the same time and enter into one another." width="2048" height="1152" loading="lazy"><figcaption>The five stages of creation — structure and process (Ver.1.3)</figcaption></figure>

<div class="reader-linkcards">
<a class="reader-linkcard glow-link" href="https://uminomae.github.io/project-design-theory/cs/">
<img class="reader-linkcard__thumb" src="../assets/thumbs/creation-space.jpg" alt="" width="720" height="378" loading="lazy">
<span class="reader-linkcard__body"><span class="reader-linkcard__title">What Is Creation (in Japanese)</span><span class="reader-linkcard__sub">Exploring the five stages of creation (field → wave → edge → vortex → bundle) across 30 academic domains.</span><span class="reader-linkcard__url">uminomae.github.io/project-design-theory/cs</span></span>
</a>
</div>

pjdhiro sees this shape of creation as closest to the image of a great ocean and its waves. pjdhiro also sees the vortex as the fluctuation of the field itself taking shape, something that cannot be cut out of the field and taken out.

<details><summary>More — examples from five domains where similar shapes have been reported</summary>

Here are examples of similar shapes, choosing five representative domains from the 30 domains looked into. Other domains, too, have many examples of similar shapes. The sources do not write in five stages; Claude analyzed the descriptions of the theories and arranged them in the order of the five stages.

- A plain example (romantic love): The field is two people who do not know each other, each in their own life. The wave is the two meeting and being attracted to each other. The edge is, the closer they get, differences in ways of thinking and living, and the difference between the partner one pictured and the partner in front of one, colliding. The vortex is the two becoming as if one while each remains a different person. The bundle is the life and promises the two built up together remaining and becoming the field of the days to come. Research on couples at the start of marriage is placed in the fold below.
- Management studies (innovation, and the evolution of organizations):
  - Innovation (Schumpeter's new combinations): The field is the circular flow of an economy repeating familiar routes. The wave is the person who tries to leave the familiar route and do something new, the resistance of surrounding society, and resistance within oneself. The edge is pulling means of production away from old uses and fighting competitors head on. The vortex is newly combining and using what was pulled away (new combinations). The bundle is new combinations occurring in swarms, disrupting the existing system and forcing adaptation, with revolution and the absorption of its results alternating.
  - Innovation (organizational knowledge creation, Nonaka et al.): The field is the organization and the project (months to years). The wave is pairs that look contradictory, such as tacit and explicit knowledge, or order and chaos. The edge is routines, habits and cognitive frameworks breaking down through change from outside or difficult challenges from above (fluctuation), and a sense of crisis arising. The vortex is a concept being born in dialogue and, after justification, becoming an archetype. The bundle is the archetype moving to another department or level and starting the next cycle.
  - The evolution of organizations (March's exploration and exploitation): The field is the organization's knowledge (the code) and the individuals belonging to it. The wave is the exploration of new possibilities and the exploitation of known certainties. The edge is the clash of gains between the short and long term, and between individual and organizational knowledge; the code can learn only from individuals who deviate from it. The vortex is slow-learning individuals and turnover keeping diversity, and the knowledge of the code rising. The bundle is the balance between exploration and exploitation being kept, and the organization surviving and prospering.
- Psychology (moving between good and bad aspects; Klein): The field is the infant's mind. The wave is love and hate splitting between times of being satisfied and not, and protecting oneself from the terrifying aspect by enlarging the good aspect (idealization). The edge is the two aspects held apart colliding, and fear of loss, a sense of guilt and feelings close to mourning arising. The vortex is the loved aspect and the hated aspect being felt as belonging to one person who is not split apart. The urge to repair the one one feels one has harmed (reparation) comes to the fore, trust is regained little by little, and while bearing the knowledge that the other was not perfect, one can keep trust and love. The bundle is the splitting movement remaining in changed form, and anxious situations being experienced again and again.
- Biochemistry (two, from life science and chemistry):
  - Fertilization (life science; from research on mouse zygotes): The field is the inside of the egg where fertilization happens. The wave is the half genomes (maternal and paternal) that the two gametes, split into female and male, the egg and the sperm, each bring. The edge is the chromosomes of the two genomes meeting for the first time when the nuclear membrane breaks down and the first division begins. The vortex is a spindle forming around each of the two genomes, the two lining up and carrying out one division together while the two genomes remain distinguishable without mixing. The bundle is becoming a two-cell embryo, and the two genomes mixing in the following divisions to become the genome of a new individual.
  - Chemistry (the BZ reaction): The field is the solution in one vessel (bromate ions, an organic acid and a catalyst dissolved in it). The wave is two substances competing for an intermediate. The edge is the switch to another process when one falls below a critical concentration, a process that indirectly brings it back. The vortex is neither process winning outright, and the back-and-forth remaining as a stable form. The bundle is arriving at the same single periodic orbit regardless of the initial state.
- Physics (dissipative structures, with Bénard convection as the example): The field is a layer of liquid heated from below. Small fluctuations away from the average state keep arising and dying out. The wave is some fluctuations growing once the temperature gradient exceeds a certain value. The edge is the contest between buoyancy and the viscosity and heat conduction that hold it back; once the value is exceeded, of the many patterns of disturbance, the one that grows fastest wins out. The vortex is the grown fluctuation becoming an order sustained by exchange of energy with the outside, the layer dividing into cells. The bundle is scattered polygons slowly settling into a network of regular hexagons.

Cases that departed from each shape are also on record. In the wave of new combinations, businesses that cannot adapt may perish. An organization leaning only toward exploration scatters immature ideas; one leaning only toward exploitation is trapped in a suboptimal stability. In a zygote, if the two spindles fail to line up, the result is an embryo of cells with two nuclei. In the BZ reaction, when conditions are off, no oscillation happens and it settles into a steady state. In Bénard convection, if the temperature gradient is smaller than the dividing value, fluctuations die out and things return to how they were.

The records of the other 25 domains also list theories arranged in the same columns. Not every theory fits, however; shapes that did not reach integration and theories that did not fit are also on record.

<details><summary>More — the original sources, and the records for each field</summary>

These are the original sources of the five cases, and the records for each field that read them. The content of the theories, and the reading of whether they resemble the five stages, are in the records. The list of records for the 30 fields opens in the window of [What Is Creation](https://uminomae.github.io/project-design-theory/cs/?modal=domains) (in Japanese). pjdhiro chose the five fields and themes, and Claude chose which theories to take up. All the original sources were checked against their full text.

- Physics (dissipative structures): [Prigogine (1977)](https://www.nobelprize.org/uploads/2018/06/prigogine-lecture.pdf), [Rayleigh (1916)](https://doi.org/10.1080/14786441608635602), [Bénard (1901)](https://archive.org/details/lestourbillonsce00bena). Theory 8 in [the physics record](https://github.com/uminomae/project-design-theory/blob/main/cs/evidence/evidence-D02-physics.md), theory 1 in [the complexity science record](https://github.com/uminomae/project-design-theory/blob/main/cs/evidence/evidence-D29-complexity-science.md) (in Japanese).
- Life science (fertilization): [Reichmann et al. (2018)](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.aar7462).
- Chemistry (the BZ reaction): [Field & Noyes (1974)](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.1681288). Theory 3 in [the chemistry record](https://github.com/uminomae/project-design-theory/blob/main/cs/evidence/evidence-D03-chemistry.md) (in Japanese).
- Psychology (Klein): Klein (1946, [1940](https://archive.org/details/ijp-xxi-1940-2)). The difference between idealization and trust is looked at in the chapter "[How do idealization and trust differ?](#ldd-idealize)".
- A plain case (romantic love): the research follows mainly couples at the start of marriage. [Niehuis et al. (2013)](https://www.bgsu.edu/content/dam/BGSU/college-of-arts-and-sciences/NCFMR/documents/WP/WP-13-05.pdf), [Murray et al. (2011)](https://doi.org/10.1177/0956797611403155).
- Management studies (innovation): Schumpeter ([1912](https://doi.org/10.3790/978-3-428-51746-6), [1939](https://archive.org/details/in.ernet.dli.2015.150123), [1942](https://archive.org/details/in.ernet.dli.2015.190072)) is theory 1 in [the economics record](https://github.com/uminomae/project-design-theory/blob/main/cs/evidence/evidence-D21-economics.md), and Nonaka et al. ([1996](https://doi.org/10.1016/0160-791X(96)00001-2), [2000](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0024-6301(99)00115-6)) theory 1 in [the business management record](https://github.com/uminomae/project-design-theory/blob/main/cs/evidence/evidence-D22-business-management.md) (in Japanese).
- Management studies (organizational evolution): [March (1991)](https://doi.org/10.1287/orsc.2.1.71). Theory 5 in the business management record.

Under art, myth is also included: the story in the first volume of the *Kojiki* in which Izanagi and Izanami walk around the heavenly pillar and give birth to the land (the original is in *Kokushi taikei* vol. 7, 1898, pp.10–11, [National Diet Library Digital Collections](https://dl.ndl.go.jp/pid/991097)). Lining it up on the five stages is Claude's reading. pjdhiro touches on this story in [Applying a folk tale to understand the spinor field](https://uminomae.github.io/pjdhiro/20250616baToMinwa/) (2025, in Japanese).

</details>

</details>

Yet what "What Is Creation" asks about is the structure and process of how things come into being. What produces the waves until something swirls into a vortex — that is, the driving source — is not asked there.

Then how do the vortices that can be seen and have names relate to the waves and the field beneath them? pjdhiro sees words and symbols as one-sided cuttings that point to vortices of the field, and sees vortices as continuous with waves, and waves with the field. It is not that a vortex has a cause for wanting to become a vortex; the sea produces the waves (the fold "[Words are names for the vortices seen between the waves](#ldd-vortex)"). Try reading the consciousness, trust, value and vision that appear later as names given to vortices between the waves. What Love-Driven Development tries to see is what gives rise to those waves.

<details class="pd-column"><summary>Words are names for the vortices seen between the waves</summary>

Where do words fall within the shape of creation? When Claude asked whether words and symbols are the shapes that rise into consciousness, pjdhiro answered as follows.

<a id="ldd-vortex"></a><strong>pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):</strong>Normally, language, symbols and numbers are cut-outs of one aspect. They point at the vortices of the field. But vortices are continuous with waves, and waves with the field. In terms of the sea, the vortices of the sea can be separated visually, but it is not that a vortex has a cause of its own for wanting to become a vortex; it is because the sea produces waves.

pjdhiro condenses the five stages of creation seen in this chapter as "the field and its fluctuation give rise to vortices". What words, symbols and numbers can cut out is the world at the size people could perceive: the vortices seen between the waves.

<details><summary>More — vortices are continuous with waves, and waves with the sea</summary>

Following what produces the waves until something swirls into a vortex means changing scale. What the management studies table looked at — markets, organizations, groups, the human mind — were all of a size people can see with their eyes and talk about in words.

In the sea analogy, vortices are continuous with waves, and waves with the sea. Then what produces the waves? Most waves on the sea are raised by wind. That wind arises from the unevenness with which the sun warms the atmosphere. The tides are caused by the pull of the moon and the sun. If you look at a vortex and think it turns by its own power, you miss what produces the waves.

What people gave names to were forms easy to perceive. Then what decides how easy something is to perceive? Visible light is a very narrow band within electromagnetic waves. People have given color names only within that band. A bat that explores its surroundings with ultrasound probably cuts the world into entirely different shapes. How the world is cut out is decided by how the one cutting receives it.

</details>

<details class="pd-column"><summary>Water and waves are not apart (an old Eastern analogy)</summary>

<a id="ldd-water"></a>Eastern thought has an analogy of the same shape. *The Awakening of Faith in the Mahāyāna* (Paramārtha's translation) cites the water of the great sea rising into waves in the wind, and writes that the aspect of water and the aspect of wind are not apart. Another translation (Śikṣānanda's) writes that the water of the sea and its waves are neither one nor different things. It is a view that even when we divide something into two and name them, they are not apart.

In this treatise, the wind is an analogy for delusion (ignorance), which points in a different direction from this page's driving source. Claude checked the quotation in the Taishō canon; pjdhiro has not read the source.

<!--REF:Eastern thought-->

Sources: [*The Awakening of Faith in the Mahāyāna*, Paramārtha's translation](https://21dzk.l.u-tokyo.ac.jp/SAT2018/satdb2018pre.php?mode=detail&ob=1&mode2=2&useid=1666_,32,0576c) (Taishō vol. 32 No.1666, p.576c, "如大海水因風波動，水相風相不相捨離") and [Śikṣānanda's translation](https://21dzk.l.u-tokyo.ac.jp/SAT2018/satdb2018pre.php?mode=detail&ob=1&mode2=2&useid=1667_,32,0585b) (Taishō vol. 32 No.1667, p.585b, "如海水與波非一非異") (both full text checked against the SAT Daizōkyō Text Database).

</details>

<details class="pd-column"><summary>From classical physics to quantum theory</summary>

Physics, too, has an example of changing scale. At the end of the nineteenth century, the theories of mechanics, heat, light and electromagnetism had achieved great success, and some thought few problems remained. Following the small discrepancies that remained and peering inside the atom, everyday words such as position and speed did not work as they were. Even so, understanding that minute world became the explanation of the large world: why things are hard, why the sun keeps shining.

Following the driving source is a change of scale in the same direction. From the size of named vortices such as markets, organizations and minds, it goes down to the waves and the field beneath them. What is similar is the shape: in a layer where the words of visible size hardly work, there may be something that moves the large world. pjdhiro, too, says that at the end of dividing oneself in shikan, it becomes "a different sense, as if changing from classical to quantum" (the fold "[Seeing the invisible center by dividing oneself](#ldd-center)").

<details><summary>More — from the two clouds to quantum theory</summary>

In 1900, Lord Kelvin, in a lecture, named "two clouds" hanging over the theories of heat and light. One was that no evidence could be found of the earth moving through the ether that was thought to carry light. The other was that the theory of equipartition of energy did not agree with the specific heats of gases and solids. In the same year, to explain the distribution of colors of light given off by hot bodies, Planck assumed that energy is exchanged in discrete lumps. The first cloud later became one of the problems that led to the theory of relativity, and the second, alongside Planck's work on thermal radiation, became one of the starting points of quantum theory.

In the world of quantum theory, the position and momentum of a particle cannot both be fixed as precisely as one likes at the same time (the uncertainty principle). Electrons behave both like grains and like waves. Intuitions built from everyday words are betrayed here again and again.

Even so, understanding this minute world explained the large world: atoms keeping their shape without collapsing (the uncertainty principle); things having volume and being hard even when many atoms gather (the Pauli exclusion principle); atoms binding into molecules (the chemical bond); hydrogen turning into helium at the center of the sun so that it keeps shining (the tunnel effect makes that reaction easier to occur). Semiconductors, and the computers we use now, are built on this understanding too.

</details>

<!--REF:Physics and the history of science-->

Sources: [Kelvin (1901)](https://doi.org/10.1080/14786440109462664) "Nineteenth Century Clouds over the Dynamical Theory of Heat and Light", *Phil. Mag.* 6th ser. 2:1–40 (lecture at the Royal Institution on 27 April 1900; full text checked against [the scan of the journal pages held by Smithsonian Libraries on the Internet Archive](https://archive.org/details/londonedinburgh621901lond) (public domain); the two clouds pp.1–2, the ether experiments p.7, specific heats p.18) / [Passon (2021)](https://doi.org/10.1119/10.0005620) "Kelvin's clouds", *Am. J. Dtsch. Phys. Amer. Math. Rev.* 55:434 (full text checked against [the Wayback Machine record of the publisher APS's PDF](https://web.archive.org/web/20260908235523/https://journals.aps.org/pr/pdf/10.1103/PhysRev.55.434) (8 September 2026); abstract on p.434).

</details>

<details class="pd-column"><summary>Particles were ways the field vibrates</summary>

In the minute world of physics, too, the way names are given has something in common with vortices between the waves. In quantum field theory, electrons and photons are treated as one of the ways a field vibrates (an excitation). The name electron is a word people gave to a certain way a field vibrates. What is similar is that a name has been given to a state that cannot be cut off from the field (it does not mean that an electron has the shape of a vortex).

In this view, when the field vibrates a particle appears, and when the vibration subsides the particle disappears. Even in a vacuum that seems to hold nothing, the field fluctuates (one of the beginnings was Dirac in 1927, who wrote the photon as a lump of vibration of the electromagnetic field).

Picture a single electron as a small, independent ball on its own, and you stumble in this world. An electron is both something counted as one grain and a name people gave to a certain way a field vibrates. The names awareness and trust, which come later, can be read the same way: as names given to ways of vibrating that cannot be cut off from the field.

<!--REF:Physics and the history of science-->

Sources: [Dirac (1927)](https://doi.org/10.1098/rspa.1927.0039) "The Quantum Theory of the Emission and Absorption of Radiation", *Proc. R. Soc. A* 114:243 (full text checked against [the Wayback Machine record of the publisher Royal Society's PDF](https://web.archive.org/web/20210921014432/https://royalsocietypublishing.org/doi/pdf/10.1098/rspa.1927.0039) (21 September 2021); pp.243–245).

</details>

</details>

## Isn't Love like gravity?

<!--OPEN-->

In the shape of creation, the field fluctuates, waves rise, and vortices swirl between the waves. Then what do that field, and the driving source that makes the vortices swirl, resemble? To pjdhiro, gravity felt close. Seeing that Love also seems related to something like a gravitational field, pjdhiro placed the following hypothesis.

<a id="ldd-well"></a><strong>pjdhiro's hypothesis (translated by Claude):</strong>In a field where waves appear as if made of grains of love, a Love field, the universe and we drift as if drawn by the distortions of the field of Love.

To the two faces of Love set out in Part 1, the force one feels drawn by and the character of a field, pjdhiro adds, as an analogy, the relation between gravity and spacetime. Gravity is felt as a force that draws things, and in general relativity it is also written as a character of a field: the distortion of spacetime around a mass. Stars and light alike travel along that distortion, and to those inside, the distortion itself cannot be seen. The mortar-shaped picture of that hollow is called a **gravity well** on this site. Dots connecting within one person, being moved by fluctuations of the field, and people gathering and something beginning to be born may all, to put it as an analogy, happen within that distortion.

On the left is a gravity well; on the right, a well of the same shape with love placed at its center. The figures can be rotated by dragging. The figure on the right is an analogy.

Stand at the bottom of the well, and everything around appears to revolve around you. Yet there is one place that shows up in no direction you face: this very center you are looking from. Its shape resembles how what moves a person is hard to see for the one being moved.

This analogy also has places where it does not fit. Gravity draws every thing in the same way. Where people are drawn differs from person to person, and changes over time even in the same person.

<details><summary>More — the gravitational field and the gravity well</summary>

Here, gravity is seen as a gravitational field. A gravitational field is distorted around a mass. General relativity writes this distortion as the curvature of spacetime. In Newtonian mechanics, the gravitational field around a mass can be drawn as the depth of a hollow (the gravitational potential).

The picture that makes this hollow three-dimensional is the gravity well. What the picture depicts, and how far it is fact of physics and where it becomes an analogy, are gathered in [What is a gravity well?](gravity-well.html) (in Japanese).

What this reader borrows from gravity is the shape in which a force that draws things can also be written as a distortion of a field. It is borrowed to look side by side at Love's two faces: the force one feels drawn by, and the character of a field.

</details>

<details><summary>More — seeing the invisible center by dividing oneself</summary>

<a id="ldd-center"></a>

This part proceeds in three steps: the center that cannot be seen from oneself, dividing oneself in trying to see it, and what is felt at the end of that dividing.

First, what pjdhiro was looking for.

<strong>pjdhiro's background (translated by Claude):</strong>As the energy that goes field → wave → edge → vortex until it swirls into a vortex, I am looking for a driving engine like a void, a center that is zero and does nothing, like gravity or love.

In the figure of the gravity well, the center never moves once ([three properties of the center](gravity-well.html#gw-center), in Japanese). Even so, every orbit in the figure follows the hollow this unmoving place has made. And, as seen in the main text, from the bottom of the well that very center does not show up.

If the center cannot be seen from oneself, seeing it means dividing the self that is looking.

<a id="ldd-shikan"></a><strong>pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):</strong>Metacognition should also be the work of dividing oneself. When you practice *shikan* (stopping and seeing), watching the inside of yourself as if pointing at it as an object, in the end the observing I remains. From trying to watch even that, the watching I and the watched I become the same. They can no longer be divided. The process from there becomes a different sense, as if changing from classical to quantum. If I were to explain metacognition, the closest analogy, I think, is the work of looking at an electron in ever finer detail.

pjdhiro also likens what kind of work shikan is in the language of programming.

<strong>pjdhiro's image (translated by Claude):</strong>Meditation is like the C function bzero(), or memset(0). It makes it easier to discern whether there is a change or not. Like polling for the 1 that something like stray thoughts sets. Shikan feels like that.

bzero() and memset(0) are functions that fill a region of memory entirely with 0. Polling is a way of going to look, repeatedly, at whether a state has changed. About what one goes to look at, pjdhiro continued as follows.

<strong>pjdhiro's view (translated by Claude):</strong>It is the work of catching thoughts, feelings and thinking that arise, and sounds and images I perceive, and making them objects one after another. Sensory stimuli such as perceived sounds and images are less made into objects than a matter of looking at the I that caught them. Feel it as it is, and confirm that there is an I that is feeling. It isn't something difficult like objectifying; you point at it with your heart.

At the end of dividing, the layer changes. The I that envisions, plans and decides can be written in classical terms. When that I is divided, the observing I and the observed I can no longer be divided, and from there on it becomes a different sensation. What is felt beyond that, pjdhiro writes as follows.

<strong>pjdhiro's image (translated by Claude):</strong>……After only the one who observes and the one observed remain, rather than a sense of stability from the two fusing, what is close is a feeling of calm, as if the presence of a mother, connected by an umbilical cord to the top of the head of the I that can only look, brings stability. ……Boundlessness is the sense that what lies beyond cannot be seen. As an expression I used the analogy of the umbilical cord and the mother, but a fetus probably does not recognize that there is a mother in the first place. In the same way, the field or the great ocean is something one does not know. If you include the circulation of the nutrients and oxygen the mother takes in, what lies beyond is connected with the whole universe.

One cannot see ahead, and yet one is stable. Apart from the reassurance gained by confirming, there is also a reassurance of being connected without confirming. The primary source for this flow is [chapter 2](gravity-love.html#gl-center) to [chapter 4](gravity-love.html#gl-manager) of the sister reader "Isn't Love like gravity?" (in Japanese).

</details>

## How far does it resemble physics in structure?

<!--OPEN-->

<a id="ldd-physics"></a>The question of this chapter is how far what is felt in the mind and the shapes that physics draws have the same structure. As an analogy, take heat. When you go into a sauna, your skin feels hot. The feeling of hot is one. In the latter half of the nineteenth century, physics explained that temperature as the average of the motion of countless invisible gas molecules. Beneath one feeling, the motion of countless molecules turned out to be going on.

What this analogy is meant to say is two things. The first is that even when an explanation of the layer below is found, the feeling of the layer above does not disappear. Even after the motion of molecules was understood, the feeling of hot remained as it was; the explanation only told us what the body had been receiving. So setting physics and the mind side by side does not become a story that the mind is nothing but the motion of molecules. The second is that beneath a feeling there may be a layer not yet seen. Beneath the feelings of being drawn, or of something welling up, might there also be something like what the motion of molecules is for heat? This is pjdhiro's estimate, placed below. Whether it follows the same laws as physics is not yet known. This chapter first looks at how far the shapes resemble each other.

Physics, too, has a way of seeing one thing from several shapes. The physicist Bohm, in his textbook *Quantum Theory* (1951), cited the story of [the blind men and the elephant](#ldd-blind-elephant) to explain that light behaves like particles in some situations and like waves in others. Just as the one idea of an elephant brings together the blind men's separate experiences, he asks whether one idea can be found that brings together the separate experiences of light. The shapes of physics lined up in this chapter, too, are not set out with any one of them as Love's answer, but as each touching one face of the invisible driving source.

The two chapters of Part 2 borrowed shapes from physics: the [gravity well](gravity-well.html), the change of scale from classical to quantum touched on in a fold, and the vortices between the waves and the fluctuation of the field. This chapter gathers the borrowed shapes in one place, and sets out which shape of physics each of the four explorations this reader is built from seems to overlap with.

- The driving source, with the center of the gravity well: the center that moves things is hard to see from what it moves.
- Kesson, with the direction of a wave (its phase): it does not show up in numbers on its own, and yet it works.
- Awareness, with measuring: while measuring, the measuring side and the measured side are written together as one.
- Creation, with the fluctuation of the field: a named vortex cannot be cut off from the field.

Each is a shape in which something unseen decides a movement that is seen. The physics side is placed in the fold "More — the four explorations and the shapes of physics".

They can be borrowed because there is one estimate beneath them.

<strong>pjdhiro's estimate (translated by Claude):</strong>Abstractly, and essentially, my estimate is: don't the wave motion that makes up matter and love in the broad sense follow the same laws of physics? To give an analogy, the wave motion of gravity and elementary particles may be happening in the human body too, and so on. It's a fantasy, though.

What pjdhiro is thinking is that something quantum-theoretical may be happening in the neurotransmitters transmitted to the brain's nerves and in the processing of signals that follows. The mind, as an apparatus of interpretation, perceives that as love and emotion. The estimate is that human cognition and interpretation may be made that way.

Whether it is the same law is left before the point of checking. What keeps being asked is how far the structures resemble each other. Whether quantum effects work in the brain is being studied in quantum biology, and there is no answer yet (the fold "Taking the estimate literally").

<details><summary>More — the four explorations and the shapes of physics</summary>

**The driving source and gravity.** In the figure of the gravity well, the things around revolve along the hollow made by the mass at the center. Where it overlaps with the driving source of the previous chapter is that the center that moves things is hard to see from what it moves.

**Kesson and the phase of waves.** A wave in quantum mechanics has a size and a direction (its phase). The probability of finding the particle at a place is set by the size of the wave (the square of its absolute value), and the direction does not enter. But when two waves overlap, the difference in direction shows up clearly as interference fringes. The phase itself does not show up in numbers on its own, yet where waves overlap it produces a definite difference.

This is where it overlaps with the shape of kesson-driven thinking: a value that is 0 in cognition and yet works (seen as the analogy of the imaginary axis in the Part 3 chapter "[When signals and thoughts part, one feels a lack](#ldd-kesson)"). What overlaps is the behavior. A value on the imaginary axis is a magnitude whose projection onto the axis of action is 0. Phase is a direction that disappears when the absolute value is taken. The reasons they do not show up in numbers differ, but they share not showing up in numbers on their own and yet working.

**Awareness and observation.** In the quantum world, measuring can change the state of what is measured. For example, measuring position disturbs momentum. In models of measurement, during measurement the object being measured and a small part of the apparatus (the probe) are taken together as one system, and its change over time is written. After the measurement, only the probe side is measured and read off on the scale. The model writes only this far; it does not write that the observing side and the observed side become inseparable. This page sees this form, writing the two together during measurement, as an analogy overlapping with where metacognition, dividing oneself, ends up.

**Creation and the fluctuations of the field.** In quantum field theory, particles appear as one of the ways a field vibrates (an excitation). Separately, as a matter of fluids, in superfluid helium the flow around a vortex (circulation) appears only in fixed units. In fluids, there are examples where the vortex itself appears as a form that cannot be cut off from the fluid. Both overlap with the shape of creation in which a named vortex cannot be taken out of the field.

<!--REF:Physics and the history of science-->

R. Soc. A* 260:218 (quantization of superfluid vortices; full text checked against the Wayback Machine record (26 October 2019) of the publisher Royal Society's PDF; abstract on p.218).

</details>

<details><summary>More — the mind as an apparatus of interpretation</summary>

The view that the mind interprets signals arising in the body and senses them as emotions also exists in research on emotion. Barrett's theory of constructed emotion holds that the brain interprets signals from inside the body with concepts it has learned so far, and constructs emotions on the spot. Its explanation is that the same sensation can have many causes.

In this reader's words: the body receives the magnitude of the value itself, and the mind, as an apparatus of interpretation, projects it and reads it as love or anxiety. How the same unseen beyond is read changes with the signals that are welling up.

<!--REF:Nerves and emotion-->

Sources: [Barrett (2017)](https://doi.org/10.1093/scan/nsw154) "The theory of constructed emotion", *Social Cognitive and Affective Neuroscience* 12(1):1 (full text checked against the full text at [PMC5390700](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5390700/); the passage on constructing emotions by categorizing sensations with concepts in the section "The computational architecture of the brain is a conceptual system plus pattern generators", the passage that the same sensation can have many causes in the glossary).

</details>

<details><summary>Taking the estimate literally — do quanta work in the nerves?</summary>

pjdhiro feels that some things are so similar that one wonders whether they are the same law, and that if it were the same law, it would, if anything, be easier to believe.

The human body, too, is made of matter, and it is hard to think of it as outside the laws of physics. However, how far quantum effects work in the processing of neural signals has not yet been answered even among researchers.

Quantum effects working inside living things are studied as quantum biology. For example, it is known that the tunnel effect, in which particles slip through barriers, is involved in enzyme reactions.

For the brain, there are proposals, the first experiments of the 2020s, and calculations against. Current neuroscience writes the release and reception of neurotransmitters, and the electrical signals that follow, almost entirely in classical physics. A review of quantum effects in the brain, too, sums up that research mostly stays at theory. Where things stand now is that whether quantum effects work in the processing of neural signals has been neither confirmed nor fully ruled out. No peer-reviewed study has yet been found that examines emotion or love itself in connection with quantum effects in the brain. pjdhiro's estimate is left before this checking.

<details><summary>More — the proposals, experiments and calculations one by one</summary>

For the brain, there are several proposals. Penrose and Hameroff proposed that quantum processes proceed in the microtubules inside neurons, and that each time they spontaneously collapse under certain conditions, a moment of consciousness arises. Fisher proposed that the nuclear spins of phosphorus atoms may be able to hold quantum information for a long time in the brain. There are also proposals and calculations that quantum coherence inside ion channels may be involved in how ions pass through.

In the 2020s, experiments also appeared. Babcock and colleagues put microtubules taken from pig brains in solution and studied them, predicting in theory and confirming in experiment that the tryptophan lined up inside microtubules forms a state that, on receiving ultraviolet light, glows together as one (superradiance). This is a result in the test tube; that light given off by the brain's metabolism may pass through this state and carry information quickly is the authors' outlook. Khan and colleagues showed that giving rats a drug that stabilizes microtubules delayed loss of consciousness under anesthetic gas by 69 seconds on average, and suggested that the anesthetic gas binding to microtubules makes consciousness be lost. They did not measure quantum effects themselves. Closer to mood, Ettenberg and colleagues reported that, in rats in a model of mania, isotopes of lithium with different nuclear spins (⁶Li and ⁷Li) worked differently, and at the end of the discussion put the reading that the difference in nuclear spin may be the cause. In the human brain, Kerskens and López Pérez suggested from MRI signals that brain functions involved in consciousness may be mediating quantum entanglement.

There are also calculations and objections against. Tegmark estimated that in the brain at body temperature, quantum superpositions break down in an extremely short time, falling far short of the speed of neural signals. Player and Hore calculated that, against the entanglement lasting hours that Fisher's proposal needs, the upper limit of its lifetime even under ideal conditions is 37 minutes, and likely much shorter in practice. Against the MRI signals of Kerskens and colleagues, Warren objected that they can be explained by classical physics, and that a collection of spins close to thermal equilibrium at body temperature cannot become entangled.

</details>

<!--REF:Nerves and emotion-->
